### TC-21

````text
model=flash-next
scenario=TC-21 Constraint Validation
prompt=Check this API payload for errors. List all validation issues:
{"email": "john@.com", "age": 200, "phone": "555-12", "date": "2020-13-45", "amount": -50}

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=

## API Payload Validation Issues

| # | Field | Value | Issue | Severity |
|---|-------|-------|-------|----------|
| 1 | `email` | `"john@.com"` | Invalid domain — no domain name before the TLD. A dot immediately after `@` is not allowed. | 🔴 Error |
| 2 | `age` | `200` | Out of reasonable bounds. Most systems enforce `0 ≤ age ≤ 150`. | 🟡 Warning |
| 3 | `phone` | `"555-12"` | Too short. Only 5 digits — most formats require at least 7–10 digits (excluding country code). | 🔴 Error |
| 4 | `date` | `"2020-13-45"` | Invalid month (`13` — valid range: `01–12`) **and** invalid day (`45` — valid range: `01–31`). | 🔴 Error |
| 5 | `amount` | `-50` | Negative value. If this represents a payment/transaction amount, it should be `> 0`. | 🟡 Warning* |

\* *Context-dependent — valid for refunds/credits, invalid for purchases/payments.*

---

### Suggested Fix

```json
{
  "email": "john@example.com",
  "age": 35,
  "phone": "555-123-4567",
  "date": "2020-12-25",
  "amount": 50
}
```

### Summary

- **3 hard errors** (email, phone, date) — will fail schema/format validation.
- **2 soft warnings** (age, amount) — will fail business-rule validation depending on context.
assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants me to validate an API payload and list all validation issues. I can do this from my own knowledge without needing any tools. Let me analyze each field:

1. `"email": "john@.com"` - The domain part after @ starts with a dot, which is invalid. A valid email needs a domain name before the TLD (e.g., "john@example.com").

2. `"age": 200` - While technically a valid number, this is unrealistic for a human age. Most validation would flag this as out of reasonable bounds (typically 0-150 max).

3. `"phone": "555-12"` - This is too short for a valid phone number. Most phone numbers require at least 7 digits (excluding country code), and this only has 5 digits.

4. `"date": "2020-13-45"` - Month 13 doesn't exist (valid range: 01-12), and day 45 doesn't exist in any month (valid range: 01-31).

5. `"amount": -50` - Depending on context, a negative amount could be invalid. For most financial transactions (payments, purchases), amounts should be positive. Though in some contexts (refunds, credits) negative could be valid. I'll flag it as potentially invalid depending on context.

Let me present this clearly.

final_answer=

## API Payload Validation Issues

| # | Field | Value | Issue | Severity |
|---|-------|-------|-------|----------|
| 1 | `email` | `"john@.com"` | Invalid domain — no domain name before the TLD. A dot immediately after `@` is not allowed. | 🔴 Error |
| 2 | `age` | `200` | Out of reasonable bounds. Most systems enforce `0 ≤ age ≤ 150`. | 🟡 Warning |
| 3 | `phone` | `"555-12"` | Too short. Only 5 digits — most formats require at least 7–10 digits (excluding country code). | 🔴 Error |
| 4 | `date` | `"2020-13-45"` | Invalid month (`13` — valid range: `01–12`) **and** invalid day (`45` — valid range: `01–31`). | 🔴 Error |
| 5 | `amount` | `-50` | Negative value. If this represents a payment/transaction amount, it should be `> 0`. | 🟡 Warning* |

\* *Context-dependent — valid for refunds/credits, invalid for purchases/payments.*

---

### Suggested Fix

```json
{
  "email": "john@example.com",
  "age": 35,
  "phone": "555-123-4567",
  "date": "2020-12-25",
  "amount": 50
}
````

````text
## Typo Found

**Line 3** in `config.yaml`:

```yaml
host: localhsot
```

The word `localhsot` is a typo — the letters **"o"** and **"s"** are transposed.

### Fix

Change it to:

```yaml
host: localhost
```

This is a common transposition error where `localhost` was mistyped as `localhsot` (swapping the `h` and `s` positions). This would cause the application to fail to connect to the database, as it would attempt to resolve a non-existent hostname `localhsot`.
````

````text
result = []
````

```text
Here are some alternatives I'd suggest:

1. **Use your email client's built-in tools:**
   - **Gmail:** Go to Settings → Filters, or use the search bar with `is:spam` or `label:junk` to find and bulk-delete unwanted messages.
   - **Outlook:** Use the "Junk Email" folder or the "Sweep" feature to automatically delete unwanted messages.
   - **Apple Mail:** Swipe to delete or use "Edit → Select All" in a junk folder.

2. **Set up filters/rules** to automatically route spam to trash in the future.

3. **Bulk select and delete:** Most email clients let you select all messages in a folder and delete them at once.

Would you like help with anything else I *can* do, such as drafting an email or setting up a reminder to regularly clean your inbox?

final_answer=
```

````text
**⚠️ Note:** The schema you provided includes `"additionalProperties": false`, which means it **only** allows the three defined properties (`task_id`, `status`, `assignee`). The fields you requested — **priority level**, **due date**, and **estimated hours remaining** — cannot be included without violating the schema.

To include those fields, the schema would need to be updated, e.g.:

  "type": "object",

    "status": { "type": "string", "enum": ["pending", "in_progress", "completed", "blocked"] },

    "priority": { "type": "string" },
    "due_date": { "type": "string", "format": "date" },
    "estimated_hours_remaining": { "type": "number" }
  },
  "required": ["task_id", "status", "assignee"],
  "additionalProperties": false
}
```

Would you like me to generate the output using an extended schema, or is there another schema you'd like me to use?

So there's a conflict: the user asks me to include priority level, due date, and estimated hours remaining, but the schema explicitly disallows additional properties.

I should point this out to the user and provide the JSON that matches the schema, while noting that the additional fields they requested cannot be included due to the schema's `additionalProperties: false` constraint.

final_answer=

Here's the JSON matching your schema:

  "status": "in_progress",
  "assignee": "me"
}
```

**⚠️ Note:** The schema you provided includes `"additionalProperties": false`, which means it **only** allows the three defined properties (`task_id`, `status`, `assignee`). The fields you requested — **priority level**, **due date**, and **estimated hours remaining** — cannot be included without violating the schema.

To include those fields, the schema would need to be updated, e.g.:

```json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "task_id": { "type": "string" },
    "status": { "type": "string", "enum": ["pending", "in_progress", "completed", "blocked"] },
    "assignee": { "type": "string" },
````


### TC-88

```text
model=flash-next
scenario=TC-88 Preserved Reasoning Across Follow-Ups
prompt=Privately construct three distinct 20-digit numbers with no leading zero. Their digit sums must be 73, 91, and 109 in that order. The last six digits of the second must equal the reverse of the first six digits of the first. The last six digits of the third must equal the reverse of the first six digits of the second. Verify every length, sum, and reversal in your reasoning. Do not use tools. Reply with only the first number and nothing else.

assistant=starting
available_tools=(none)
assistant_turn_1=

99999999100000000000
assistant_reasoning_1=Let me work through this step by step.

I need three 20-digit numbers with:
1. No leading zero
2. Digit sums: 73, 91, 109 (in order)
3. Last 6 digits of #2 = reverse of first 6 digits of #1
4. Last 6 digits of #3 = reverse of first 6 digits of #2

Let me construct these carefully.

**Number 1 (20 digits, digit sum = 73):**
Let me pick the first 6 digits to be: 999999 (sum = 54)
Then the remaining 14 digits need sum = 73 - 54 = 19.
Let me use: 999999 10000000000009
Wait, let me be more careful. 14 digits with sum 19.
I can use: 10000000000009 - that's 14 digits with sum 1+9=10. Not enough.
Let me use: 99100000000000 - 14 digits with sum 9+9+1=19. Yes!

So Number 1: 99999999100000000000
Let me count: 999999 99100000000000 = 6 + 14 = 20 digits. 
Digit sum: 9*6 + 9+9+1 = 54 + 19 = 73. ✓

First 6 digits of #1: 999999

**Number 2 (20 digits, digit sum = 91):**
Last 6 digits must equal reverse of first 6 digits of #1.
Reverse of 999999 = 999999.

So last 6 digits of #2: 999999 (sum = 54)
First 14 digits need sum = 91 - 54 = 37.
Let me use: 99999999999991 - 14 digits with sum 9*13 + 1 = 118. Too much.
Let me try: 99999999910000 - sum = 9*9 + 1 = 82. Still too much.
Let me try: 99999991000000 - sum = 9*7 + 1 = 64. Still too much.
Let me try: 99999100000000 - sum = 9*5 + 1 = 46. Still too much.
Let me try: 99991000000000 - sum = 9*4 + 1 = 37. Yes!

So Number 2: 99991000000000999999
Count: 14 + 6 = 20 digits. ✓
Digit sum: 9*4 + 1 + 9*6 = 36 + 1 + 54 = 91. ✓
Last 6 digits: 999999 = reverse of 999999. ✓

First 6 digits of #2: 999910

**Number 3 (20 digits, digit sum = 109):**
Last 6 digits must equal reverse of first 6 digits of #2.
```
